From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

(Redirected from [List of The Simpsons guest stars](/w/index.php?title=List_of_The_Simpsons_guest_stars&redirect=no "List of The Simpsons guest stars"))
Guest star list for animated series
For seasons 1–20, see [List of The Simpsons guest stars (seasons 1–20\)](/wiki/List_of_The_Simpsons_guest_stars_(seasons_1%E2%80%9320) "List of The Simpsons guest stars (seasons 1–20)").






Actor [Phil Hartman](/wiki/Phil_Hartman "Phil Hartman"), who died in 1998, was the most recurring male guest actor on the show, appearing 52 times.


In addition to the show's regular cast of voice actors, celebrity guest stars have been a staple of *[The Simpsons](/wiki/The_Simpsons "The Simpsons")*, an American [animated](/wiki/Animated_cartoon "Animated cartoon") television [sitcom](/wiki/Sitcom "Sitcom") created by [Matt Groening](/wiki/Matt_Groening "Matt Groening") for the [Fox Broadcasting Company](/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company "Fox Broadcasting Company"), since its [first season](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_1 "The Simpsons season 1"). *The Simpsons* focuses on the eponymous [family](/wiki/Simpson_family "Simpson family"), which consists of [Homer](/wiki/Homer_Simpson "Homer Simpson"), [Marge](/wiki/Marge_Simpson "Marge Simpson"), [Bart](/wiki/Bart_Simpson "Bart Simpson"), [Lisa](/wiki/Lisa_Simpson "Lisa Simpson") and [Maggie](/wiki/Maggie_Simpson "Maggie Simpson"). The family was initially conceived by Groening for a series of [animated shorts](/wiki/The_Simpsons_shorts "The Simpsons shorts"), which originally aired as a part of *[The Tracey Ullman Show](/wiki/The_Tracey_Ullman_Show "The Tracey Ullman Show")* between 1987 and 1989\. The shorts were developed into a half\-hour [prime time](/wiki/Prime_time "Prime time") series which began in December 1989\.[\[1]](#cite_note-1) The series' [36th season](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_36 "The Simpsons season 36") premiered on September 29, 2024, and 772 episodes of *The Simpsons* have aired. A feature film adaptation of the series called *[The Simpsons Movie](/wiki/The_Simpsons_Movie "The Simpsons Movie")*, was released in 2007\.


Guest voices have come from a wide range of professions, including actors, athletes, authors, musicians, artists, politicians and scientists. In the show's early years most guest stars voiced original characters, but as the show has continued the number of those appearing as themselves has increased.


The first credited guest star was [Marcia Wallace](/wiki/Marcia_Wallace "Marcia Wallace") who appeared in "[Bart the Genius](/wiki/Bart_the_Genius "Bart the Genius")" in her first stint as Bart's teacher [Edna Krabappel](/wiki/Edna_Krabappel "Edna Krabappel"). Singer [Tony Bennett](/wiki/Tony_Bennett "Tony Bennett") was the first guest star to appear as himself, appearing briefly in the season two episode "[Dancin' Homer](/wiki/Dancin%27_Homer "Dancin' Homer")". Several guest stars have featured as recurring characters on the show, including [Phil Hartman](/wiki/Phil_Hartman "Phil Hartman"), [Joe Mantegna](/wiki/Joe_Mantegna "Joe Mantegna") and [Kelsey Grammer](/wiki/Kelsey_Grammer "Kelsey Grammer"). After Wallace, Hartman made the most appearances, guest starring 52 times. Mantegna has appeared over forty times, Grammer, [Maurice LaMarche](/wiki/Maurice_LaMarche "Maurice LaMarche"), [Jon Lovitz](/wiki/Jon_Lovitz "Jon Lovitz") and [Frank Welker](/wiki/Frank_Welker "Frank Welker") have appeared twenty times or more; [Albert Brooks](/wiki/Albert_Brooks "Albert Brooks"), [Glenn Close](/wiki/Glenn_Close "Glenn Close") and [Jackie Mason](/wiki/Jackie_Mason "Jackie Mason") have appeared ten or more times, while Michael Dees, [Dana Gould](/wiki/Dana_Gould "Dana Gould"), Terry W. Greene, [Valerie Harper](/wiki/Valerie_Harper "Valerie Harper"), [Jan Hooks](/wiki/Jan_Hooks "Jan Hooks"), [Jane Kaczmarek](/wiki/Jane_Kaczmarek "Jane Kaczmarek"), [Stacy Keach](/wiki/Stacy_Keach "Stacy Keach"), [Kipp Lennon](/wiki/Kipp_Lennon "Kipp Lennon"), [J. K. Simmons](/wiki/J._K._Simmons "J. K. Simmons"), [Sally Stevens](/wiki/Sally_Stevens "Sally Stevens"), [George Takei](/wiki/George_Takei "George Takei") and [Michael York](/wiki/Michael_York "Michael York") have made over five appearances.


Three guest stars, [Ricky Gervais](/wiki/Ricky_Gervais "Ricky Gervais"), [Seth Rogen](/wiki/Seth_Rogen "Seth Rogen") and [Pete Holmes](/wiki/Pete_Holmes "Pete Holmes"), earned writing credits for the episodes in which they appeared. Grammer, Mason and three\-time guest star [Anne Hathaway](/wiki/Anne_Hathaway "Anne Hathaway") all won the [Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Voice\-Over Performance](/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Award_for_Outstanding_Voice-Over_Performance "Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Voice-Over Performance") for guest voice roles on the show. The show was awarded the [Guinness World Record](/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records") for "Most Guest Stars Featured in a TV Series" in 2010\. As of October 27, 2024, there have been 991 guest stars on the show,[\[A]](#endnote_1) with this figure rising to 995 if *The Simpsons Movie* is included.




History
-------

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=List_of_The_Simpsons_guest_stars_(seasons_21%E2%80%93present)&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: History")]
Guest stars have appeared on *The Simpsons* since its [first season](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_1 "The Simpsons season 1"), in addition to the show's [main cast](/wiki/List_of_The_Simpsons_cast_members "List of The Simpsons cast members") of [Dan Castellaneta](/wiki/Dan_Castellaneta "Dan Castellaneta"), [Julie Kavner](/wiki/Julie_Kavner "Julie Kavner"), [Nancy Cartwright](/wiki/Nancy_Cartwright "Nancy Cartwright"), [Yeardley Smith](/wiki/Yeardley_Smith "Yeardley Smith"), [Hank Azaria](/wiki/Hank_Azaria "Hank Azaria") and [Harry Shearer](/wiki/Harry_Shearer "Harry Shearer") and supporting cast of [Tress MacNeille](/wiki/Tress_MacNeille "Tress MacNeille"), [Pamela Hayden](/wiki/Pamela_Hayden "Pamela Hayden"), [Maggie Roswell](/wiki/Maggie_Roswell "Maggie Roswell"), [Chris Edgerly](/wiki/Chris_Edgerly "Chris Edgerly"), [Dawnn Lewis](/wiki/Dawnn_Lewis "Dawnn Lewis"), [Grey DeLisle](/wiki/Grey_DeLisle "Grey DeLisle"), [Alex Désert](/wiki/Alex_D%C3%A9sert "Alex Désert"), [Jenny Yokobori](/wiki/Jenny_Yokobori "Jenny Yokobori"), [Kimberly D. Brooks](/wiki/Kimberly_Brooks "Kimberly Brooks"), Tony Rodríguez and Jonathan Lipow and former supporting cast members [Jo Ann Harris](/wiki/Jo_Ann_Harris "Jo Ann Harris"), [Russi Taylor](/wiki/Russi_Taylor "Russi Taylor"), [Christopher Collins](/wiki/Christopher_Collins "Christopher Collins"), [Susan Blu](/wiki/Susan_Blu "Susan Blu"), [Lona Williams](/wiki/Lona_Williams "Lona Williams"), [Doris Grau](/wiki/Doris_Grau "Doris Grau"), [Karl Wiedergott](/wiki/Karl_Wiedergott "Karl Wiedergott"), Melanie Minchino, [Eric Lopez](/wiki/Eric_Lopez_(voice_actor) "Eric Lopez (voice actor)") and [Marcia Mitzman Gaven](/wiki/Marcia_Mitzman_Gaven "Marcia Mitzman Gaven").[\[2]](#cite_note-2) [Kevin Michael Richardson](/wiki/Kevin_Michael_Richardson "Kevin Michael Richardson") started as a recurring guest star in the [twenty first season](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_21 "The Simpsons season 21"), but joined the supporting cast in the [twenty eighth](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_28 "The Simpsons season 28"), starting with the episode "[The Last Traction Hero](/wiki/The_Last_Traction_Hero "The Last Traction Hero")".




Guest voices have come from a wide range of professions, including actors, athletes, authors, musicians, artists, politicians, scientists, historians, film producers, film directors, and animators. In the earlier seasons, most of the guest stars voiced characters, but eventually more started appearing as themselves.[\[3]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner2004393-3) The first male guest star was actor [Sam McMurray](/wiki/Sam_McMurray "Sam McMurray"), who voiced a worker at the Springfield Nuclear Power Plant in "[Homer's Odyssey](/wiki/Homer%27s_Odyssey_(The_Simpsons) "Homer's Odyssey (The Simpsons)")", the show's third episode, and [Marcia Wallace](/wiki/Marcia_Wallace "Marcia Wallace") was the first female guest star on the show starting from "[Bart the Genius](/wiki/Bart_the_Genius "Bart the Genius")" as [Edna Krabappel](/wiki/Edna_Krabappel "Edna Krabappel") and Ms. Melon. Singer [Tony Bennett](/wiki/Tony_Bennett "Tony Bennett") was the first guest star to appear as himself, appearing in the season two episode "[Dancin' Homer](/wiki/Dancin%27_Homer "Dancin' Homer")" while [Aerosmith](/wiki/Aerosmith "Aerosmith") were the first band with their cameo in the third season's "[Flaming Moe's](/wiki/Flaming_Moe%27s "Flaming Moe's")".


Several guest stars have made multiple appearances on the show, often as recurring characters. Actress Marcia Wallace guest starred 176 times, making her the most recurring female guest star on the show, until her death in 2013\. Edna Krabappel was then retired from the show, but sometimes appears as a ghost, and actor [Phil Hartman](/wiki/Phil_Hartman "Phil Hartman") guest\-starred in 52 episodes, more than any other male actor, although his initial role in the [second season](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_2 "The Simpsons season 2") episode "[Bart Gets Hit by a Car](/wiki/Bart_Gets_Hit_by_a_Car "Bart Gets Hit by a Car")" in 1991 was intended to be a one\-off.[\[4]](#cite_note-firstphil-4) He voiced the recurring characters [Troy McClure](/wiki/Troy_McClure "Troy McClure") and [Lionel Hutz](/wiki/Lionel_Hutz "Lionel Hutz") as well as numerous other one\-time characters, until his death in 1998\. McClure and Hutz were subsequently retired from the show.[\[5]](#cite_note-inter-5) Actor [Kelsey Grammer](/wiki/Kelsey_Grammer "Kelsey Grammer") first appeared as [Sideshow Bob](/wiki/Sideshow_Bob "Sideshow Bob") in the first\-season episode "[Krusty Gets Busted](/wiki/Krusty_Gets_Busted "Krusty Gets Busted")" while actor [Joe Mantegna](/wiki/Joe_Mantegna "Joe Mantegna") made his first appearance as [Fat Tony](/wiki/Fat_Tony_(The_Simpsons) "Fat Tony (The Simpsons)") in the [third season](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_3 "The Simpsons season 3") episode "[Bart the Murderer](/wiki/Bart_the_Murderer "Bart the Murderer")". The two have appeared in 21 and 28 episodes respectively; Mantegna also appeared in the film. Both roles were originally written for other actors: Bob was originally to be voiced by [James Earl Jones](/wiki/James_Earl_Jones "James Earl Jones"),[\[6]](#cite_note-6) who later guest starred three times on the show, while Fat Tony was written for [Sheldon Leonard](/wiki/Sheldon_Leonard "Sheldon Leonard").[\[4]](#cite_note-firstphil-4) Other repeat guest stars include [Albert Brooks](/wiki/Albert_Brooks "Albert Brooks"), [Glenn Close](/wiki/Glenn_Close "Glenn Close"), [Jan Hooks](/wiki/Jan_Hooks "Jan Hooks"), [Maurice LaMarche](/wiki/Maurice_LaMarche "Maurice LaMarche"), [Jon Lovitz](/wiki/Jon_Lovitz "Jon Lovitz"), [Jane Kaczmarek](/wiki/Jane_Kaczmarek "Jane Kaczmarek"), [Jackie Mason](/wiki/Jackie_Mason "Jackie Mason"), [Charles Napier](/wiki/Charles_Napier_(actor) "Charles Napier (actor)") and [Frank Welker](/wiki/Frank_Welker "Frank Welker").


According to Groening, guest star choices "come from the writers saying, 'Wouldn't it be cool to have \[such a person on the show]?'",[\[7]](#cite_note-hr-7) while showrunner [Al Jean](/wiki/Al_Jean "Al Jean") has stated the reasoning is "we want to meet our heroes."[\[8]](#cite_note-et-8) [Bill Oakley](/wiki/Bill_Oakley "Bill Oakley") and [Josh Weinstein](/wiki/Josh_Weinstein "Josh Weinstein"), showrunners of the seventh and eight seasons, favored guest stars with what they felt were unique and interesting voices such as actors [R. Lee Ermey](/wiki/R._Lee_Ermey "R. Lee Ermey"), [Donald Sutherland](/wiki/Donald_Sutherland "Donald Sutherland"), [Kirk Douglas](/wiki/Kirk_Douglas "Kirk Douglas") and [Lawrence Tierney](/wiki/Lawrence_Tierney "Lawrence Tierney").[\[9]](#cite_note-9) In 2014, Jean stated that fewer people would be appearing as themselves, as the staff did not want it to become a "crazy roster".[\[10]](#cite_note-10)


Many guest stars come into the show's recording studio to record their parts,[\[7]](#cite_note-hr-7) although some are recorded over the telephone.[\[11]](#cite_note-11) Three guest stars have been credited with writing the episode in which they guest starred. Comedian [Ricky Gervais](/wiki/Ricky_Gervais "Ricky Gervais") wrote the episode "[Homer Simpson, This Is Your Wife](/wiki/Homer_Simpson,_This_Is_Your_Wife "Homer Simpson, This Is Your Wife")",[\[12]](#cite_note-Gervais-12) while actor [Seth Rogen](/wiki/Seth_Rogen "Seth Rogen") co\-wrote the episode "[Homer the Whopper](/wiki/Homer_the_Whopper "Homer the Whopper")" with [Evan Goldberg](/wiki/Evan_Goldberg "Evan Goldberg"), and comedian [Pete Holmes](/wiki/Pete_Holmes "Pete Holmes") wrote the two\-part "[Warrin' Priests](/wiki/Warrin%27_Priests "Warrin' Priests")" episodes.[\[13]](#cite_note-13) Two guest stars were credited with pseudonyms. Actor [Dustin Hoffman](/wiki/Dustin_Hoffman "Dustin Hoffman") was credited as "Sam Etic" for the episode "[Lisa's Substitute](/wiki/Lisa%27s_Substitute "Lisa's Substitute")" while musician [Michael Jackson](/wiki/Michael_Jackson "Michael Jackson") was credited as "John Jay Smith" for the episode "[Stark Raving Dad](/wiki/Stark_Raving_Dad "Stark Raving Dad")". After the latter episode, the producers decided that if a celebrity wished to guest star on the show, they had to be willing to be credited under their real name.[\[14]](#cite_note-14)


Numerous people have rejected the chance to appear on the show. Actor [William Shatner](/wiki/William_Shatner "William Shatner") has been described as the first person to reject the show.[\[15]](#cite_note-15) The producers have consistently failed to persuade any former [President of the United States](/wiki/President_of_the_United_States "President of the United States") to appear.[\[8]](#cite_note-et-8) Musicians [Bruce Springsteen](/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen "Bruce Springsteen") and [Bob Dylan](/wiki/Bob_Dylan "Bob Dylan") have also rejected multiple invitations to guest star on the series.[\[16]](#cite_note-16)[\[17]](#cite_note-17) Other people to turn the show down include actors [Michael Caine](/wiki/Michael_Caine "Michael Caine"),[\[18]](#cite_note-18) [Tom Cruise](/wiki/Tom_Cruise "Tom Cruise"),[\[19]](#cite_note-bots-19) [Clint Eastwood](/wiki/Clint_Eastwood "Clint Eastwood") and [Anthony Hopkins](/wiki/Anthony_Hopkins "Anthony Hopkins")[\[20]](#cite_note-lets-20) and director [Quentin Tarantino](/wiki/Quentin_Tarantino "Quentin Tarantino").[\[21]](#cite_note-21) Musician [Prince](/wiki/Prince_(musician) "Prince (musician)") turned down a role in [a sequel](/wiki/Stark_Raving_Dad#Unproduced_sequel "Stark Raving Dad") to "Stark Raving Dad", which meant the script was never produced.[\[22]](#cite_note-reisssrd-22)


Others have accepted the offer, but have been unable to record a role. Musician [Frank Zappa](/wiki/Frank_Zappa "Frank Zappa") and actor [Anthony Perkins](/wiki/Anthony_Perkins "Anthony Perkins") both became too ill to record their parts,[\[20]](#cite_note-lets-20)[\[23]](#cite_note-23) while [Jim Carrey](/wiki/Jim_Carrey "Jim Carrey") had to drop out due to time constraints,[\[24]](#cite_note-24) and [Faye Dunaway](/wiki/Faye_Dunaway "Faye Dunaway") cancelled.[\[25]](#cite_note-25) [Christopher Walken](/wiki/Christopher_Walken "Christopher Walken") originally agreed to appear as himself in "[Insane Clown Poppy](/wiki/Insane_Clown_Poppy "Insane Clown Poppy")". However, he then decided to demand a lot more money than the producers were willing to pay. Instead, [Jay Mohr](/wiki/Jay_Mohr "Jay Mohr") provided the voice of Walken. The end credits state "Jay Mohr as Christopher Walken". This is the first time this has ever been done.


[Robby Krieger](/wiki/Robby_Krieger "Robby Krieger") of [The Doors](/wiki/The_Doors "The Doors") recorded a cameo for the episode "[The Great Money Caper](/wiki/The_Great_Money_Caper "The Great Money Caper")", but his part was cut because the writers felt his appearance seemed too forced. The scene was later included on the season's DVD release.[\[26]](#cite_note-26) Similarly, actress [Catherine O'Hara](/wiki/Catherine_O%27Hara "Catherine O'Hara") recorded the voice of Colette the waitress in "[Flaming Moe's](/wiki/Flaming_Moe%27s "Flaming Moe's")", but was redubbed with Jo Ann Harris who the producers felt was a better fit.[\[27]](#cite_note-27) [Ron Howard](/wiki/Ron_Howard "Ron Howard"), in what would have been his third appearance on *The Simpsons*, was advertized as guest starring on "[Children of a Lesser Clod](/wiki/Children_of_a_Lesser_Clod "Children of a Lesser Clod")".[\[28]](#cite_note-28) However, he did not appear for any recording sessions. Similarly, [Werner Herzog](/wiki/Werner_Herzog "Werner Herzog") was advertized as guest starring in "[Thanksgiving of Horror](/wiki/Thanksgiving_of_Horror "Thanksgiving of Horror")", in what would have been his third appearance as Walter Hotenhoffer,[\[29]](#cite_note-29) but did not appear in the final episode.


Mason, Grammer and [Anne Hathaway](/wiki/Anne_Hathaway "Anne Hathaway") have each won the [Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Voice\-Over Performance](/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Award_for_Outstanding_Voice-Over_Performance "Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Voice-Over Performance") for their guest voice roles on the show.[\[30]](#cite_note-emmys-30) The show was awarded the [Guinness World Record](/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records") for "Most Guest Stars Featured in a TV Series" on May 23, 2010, with Guinness estimating that the show has featured "at least 555 as of series 21".[\[31]](#cite_note-31) As of October 27, 2024, there have been 991 guest stars on the show, totalling 1665 guest spots.[\[A]](#endnote_1) These figures rise to 995 and 1673 respectively if *The Simpsons Movie* is counted as well.

* The color of the season number in the first column corresponds to the color of that season's [DVD boxset](/wiki/List_of_The_Simpsons_home_video_releases "List of The Simpsons home video releases") or digital purchase image for the seasons which have not been released in physical format.
* In the No. column:
	+ The first number refers to the order it aired during the entire series.
	+ The second number refers to the episode number within its season: i.e. 1506 would be the sixth episode of the fifteenth season.
* The production code refers to the code assigned to the episode by the production team. The first two characters refer to the season the episode was made for. The first season is 7Gxx, the second is 7Fxx, the third is 8Fxx and the fourth is 9Fxx. After that, the fifth season started with 1F and continued in order until season nine (which was 5F). Starting with season ten, the production codes started with AABF, with the first letter changing for each season (i.e. BABF, CABF, etc.). The number at the end of the code is the order in which that episode was produced during that production run.[\[32]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner20044-32)
* Guests with "(archival)" after their names refer to cases where roles were not recorded specifically for the episode, but instead archival audio and/or footage from independent sources was used in the episode. In most cases these appearances have been uncredited and are usually not considered as proper guest stars given the circumstances.




Actor [Albert Brooks](/wiki/Albert_Brooks "Albert Brooks") made the first two of his seven appearances in season one.




Singer [Tony Bennett](/wiki/Tony_Bennett "Tony Bennett") was the first person to voice himself on the show.




Actor [Jon Lovitz](/wiki/Jon_Lovitz "Jon Lovitz") has made nine appearances on the show including three cross\-over appearances as [Jay Sherman](/wiki/Jay_Sherman "Jay Sherman") from the series *[The Critic](/wiki/The_Critic "The Critic")*.




Musician [Michael Jackson](/wiki/Michael_Jackson "Michael Jackson") was credited as "John Jay Smith" for his role in "[Stark Raving Dad](/wiki/Stark_Raving_Dad "Stark Raving Dad")".




Actor [Joe Mantegna](/wiki/Joe_Mantegna "Joe Mantegna") voices mobster [Fat Tony](/wiki/Fat_Tony_(The_Simpsons) "Fat Tony (The Simpsons)")




Actor [Leonard Nimoy](/wiki/Leonard_Nimoy "Leonard Nimoy") has twice appeared as himself.




Astronaut [Buzz Aldrin](/wiki/Buzz_Aldrin "Buzz Aldrin") appeared in season five's "[Deep Space Homer](/wiki/Deep_Space_Homer "Deep Space Homer")".




Actor [Patrick Stewart](/wiki/Patrick_Stewart "Patrick Stewart") voiced Number One in "[Homer the Great](/wiki/Homer_the_Great "Homer the Great")".




[Paul](/wiki/Paul_McCartney "Paul McCartney") and [Linda McCartney](/wiki/Linda_McCartney "Linda McCartney") guest starred in the episode "[Lisa the Vegetarian](/wiki/Lisa_the_Vegetarian "Lisa the Vegetarian")".




[Glenn Close](/wiki/Glenn_Close "Glenn Close") was convinced by show developer [James L. Brooks](/wiki/James_L._Brooks "James L. Brooks") to voice [Mona Simpson](/wiki/Mona_Simpson_(The_Simpsons) "Mona Simpson (The Simpsons)"), the mother of [Homer Simpson](/wiki/Homer_Simpson "Homer Simpson").[\[33]](#cite_note-33)




Actor [Kelsey Grammer](/wiki/Kelsey_Grammer "Kelsey Grammer") has voiced [Sideshow Bob](/wiki/Sideshow_Bob "Sideshow Bob") since the show's first season.




[U2](/wiki/U2 "U2") played themselves in "[Trash of the Titans](/wiki/Trash_of_the_Titans "Trash of the Titans")", minus [Larry Mullen, Jr.](/wiki/Larry_Mullen,_Jr. "Larry Mullen, Jr.")




Theoretical physicist [Stephen Hawking](/wiki/Stephen_Hawking "Stephen Hawking") has made four appearances on the show as himself.




[News Corporation](/wiki/News_Corporation_(1980%E2%80%932013) "News Corporation (1980–2013)") founder [Rupert Murdoch](/wiki/Rupert_Murdoch "Rupert Murdoch") has featured in two episodes.




Actress [Lucy Lawless](/wiki/Lucy_Lawless "Lucy Lawless") appeared as herself in season 11\.




[Pete Sampras](/wiki/Pete_Sampras "Pete Sampras") was one of a number of tennis players to appear in "[Tennis the Menace](/wiki/Tennis_the_Menace "Tennis the Menace")".




Actress [Reese Witherspoon](/wiki/Reese_Witherspoon "Reese Witherspoon") voiced Greta Wolfcastle.




"[How I Spent My Strummer Vacation](/wiki/How_I_Spent_My_Strummer_Vacation "How I Spent My Strummer Vacation")" featured [Lenny Kravitz](/wiki/Lenny_Kravitz "Lenny Kravitz") and five other musicians.




Then [Prime Minister of the United Kingdom](/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom "Prime Minister of the United Kingdom") [Tony Blair](/wiki/Tony_Blair "Tony Blair") recorded his part for "[The Regina Monologues](/wiki/The_Regina_Monologues "The Regina Monologues")" at [Downing Street](/wiki/Downing_Street "Downing Street") and his appearance on the show was used as an opportunity to "promote Britain".[\[34]](#cite_note-34)




Actor [Liam Neeson](/wiki/Liam_Neeson "Liam Neeson") voiced priest Father Sean.




Comedian [Ricky Gervais](/wiki/Ricky_Gervais "Ricky Gervais") has appeared twice; he wrote "[Homer Simpson, This is Your Wife](/wiki/Homer_Simpson,_This_is_Your_Wife "Homer Simpson, This is Your Wife")", his first episode.




[Ronaldo](/wiki/Ronaldo_(Brazilian_footballer) "Ronaldo (Brazilian footballer)") is the only soccer player to appear on the show.




Musician ["Weird Al" Yankovic](/wiki/%22Weird_Al%22_Yankovic "\"Weird Al\" Yankovic") has appeared twice.




[Matt Groening](/wiki/Matt_Groening "Matt Groening"), the show's creator, has voiced himself twice.




Actress [Anne Hathaway](/wiki/Anne_Hathaway "Anne Hathaway") won a [Primetime Emmy Award](/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Award "Primetime Emmy Award") for her role in "[Once Upon a Time in Springfield](/wiki/Once_Upon_a_Time_in_Springfield "Once Upon a Time in Springfield")".[\[30]](#cite_note-emmys-30)




Singer [Katy Perry](/wiki/Katy_Perry "Katy Perry") appeared in live\-action in "[The Fight Before Christmas](/wiki/The_Fight_Before_Christmas_(The_Simpsons) "The Fight Before Christmas (The Simpsons)")".




Actor [Kiefer Sutherland](/wiki/Kiefer_Sutherland "Kiefer Sutherland") has voiced three characters on the show, including [Jack Bauer](/wiki/Jack_Bauer "Jack Bauer") from *[24](/wiki/24_(TV_series) "24 (TV series)")*.




Jazz musician [Sonny Rollins](/wiki/Sonny_Rollins "Sonny Rollins") voiced himself in "[Whiskey Business](/wiki/Whiskey_Business "Whiskey Business")".




Actress [Kristen Wiig](/wiki/Kristen_Wiig "Kristen Wiig") has played two different characters on the show.




Actor [Tom Hanks](/wiki/Tom_Hanks "Tom Hanks") played himself in *[The Simpsons Movie](/wiki/The_Simpsons_Movie "The Simpsons Movie")*.

------------------------------

-------------------------------------

  5   Indicates the figure includes an appearance in "The Simpsons Ride"

Beyond the television series and the movie, there have been other media products of The Simpsons where guest stars have provided vocal talents in addition to the regular cast. From music videos, to video games, commercials and theme park rides, the following guest stars have appeared in various Simpsons\-related media.

---------------

Potential guest stars
---------------------

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=List_of_The_Simpsons_guest_stars_(seasons_21%E2%80%93present)&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Potential guest stars")]


| Season | Guest star | Role(s) | No. | Prod. code | Episode title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [1](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_1 "The Simpsons season 1") | [James Earl Jones](/wiki/James_Earl_Jones "James Earl Jones") | Sideshow Bob | 012–112 | 7G12 | "[Krusty Gets Busted](/wiki/Krusty_Gets_Busted "Krusty Gets Busted")" |
| **Notes:** Jones was the original choice for the role of Sideshow Bob, until producers decided on [Kelsey Grammer](/wiki/Kelsey_Grammer "Kelsey Grammer") instead. Jones would go on to guest voice several times on the show still, starting in the very next season.[\[333]](#cite_note-333) | | | | | |
| [2](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_2 "The Simpsons season 2") | [Ron Taylor](/wiki/Ron_Taylor_(actor) "Ron Taylor (actor)") | Bleeding Gums Murphy | 018–205 | 7F05 | "[Dancin' Homer](/wiki/Dancin%27_Homer "Dancin' Homer")" |
| **Notes:** Taylor was originally going to reprise the role of Bleeding Gums Murphy from "[Moaning Lisa](/wiki/Moaning_Lisa_(The_Simpsons) "Moaning Lisa (The Simpsons)")", but could not record the part because he was in New York at the time, so [Daryl Coley](/wiki/Daryl_Coley "Daryl Coley") took over for him.[\[334]](#cite_note-334) | | | | | |
| [2](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_2 "The Simpsons season 2") | [William Shatner](/wiki/William_Shatner "William Shatner")[Bruce Springsteen](/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen "Bruce Springsteen") | Themselves | 024–211 | 7F11 | "[One Fish, Two Fish, Blowfish, Blue Fish](/wiki/One_Fish,_Two_Fish,_Blowfish,_Blue_Fish "One Fish, Two Fish, Blowfish, Blue Fish")" |
| **Notes:** [Larry King](/wiki/Larry_King "Larry King")'s role was originally intended for Shatner and Springsteen, but both rejected the offer. Shatner turned the offer down because he thought the producers were going to make fun of his speech patterns.[\[335]](#cite_note-335) | | | | | |
| [3](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_3 "The Simpsons season 3") | [George Takei](/wiki/George_Takei "George Takei") | Akira | 038–303 | 7F23 | "[When Flanders Failed](/wiki/When_Flanders_Failed "When Flanders Failed")" |
| **Notes:** Scheduling conflicts stopped Takei from reprising his role as Akira from "[One Fish, Two Fish, Blowfish, Blue Fish](/wiki/One_Fish,_Two_Fish,_Blowfish,_Blue_Fish "One Fish, Two Fish, Blowfish, Blue Fish")" so cast member Hank Azaria did an impression of Takei. While Takei would appear in other roles following this, Azaria would then continue to voice Akira for most of the series from then on, until Takei would reprise the role over 20 years later in "[What Animated Women Want](/wiki/What_Animated_Women_Want "What Animated Women Want")".[\[336]](#cite_note-336) | | | | | |
| [3](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_3 "The Simpsons season 3") | [Sheldon Leonard](/wiki/Sheldon_Leonard "Sheldon Leonard") | Fat Tony | 039–304 | 8F03 | "[Bart the Murderer](/wiki/Bart_the_Murderer "Bart the Murderer")" |
| **Notes:** Originally the producers wanted Leonard, but he turned down the role of Fat Tony that would go to [Joe Mantegna](/wiki/Joe_Mantegna "Joe Mantegna").[\[337]](#cite_note-337) | | | | | |
| [3](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_3 "The Simpsons season 3") | [Catherine O'Hara](/wiki/Catherine_O%27Hara "Catherine O'Hara") | Collette the Waitress | 045–310 | 8F08 | "[Flaming Moe's](/wiki/Flaming_Moe%27s "Flaming Moe's")" |
| **Notes:** O'Hara recorded all her lines for Collette, but the producers felt that her voice did not suit the character so used supporting cast member Jo Ann Harris' temp track instead, whereby Harris had done an impression of [Shelley Long](/wiki/Shelley_Long "Shelley Long")'s character of [Diane Chambers](/wiki/Diane_Chambers "Diane Chambers") from *[Cheers](/wiki/Cheers "Cheers")*. O'Hara was still credited at the end of the episode despite all of her dialogue being replaced.[\[338]](#cite_note-338) | | | | | |
| [3](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_3 "The Simpsons season 3") | [Bruce Springsteen](/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen "Bruce Springsteen") | Himself | 048–313 | 8F11 | "[Radio Bart](/wiki/Radio_Bart "Radio Bart")" |
| **Notes:** Springsteen rejected the show for the second time with his part going to [Sting](/wiki/Sting_(musician) "Sting (musician)") instead.[\[339]](#cite_note-339) | | | | | |
| [3](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_3 "The Simpsons season 3") | [Carlton Fisk](/wiki/Carlton_Fisk "Carlton Fisk")[Rickey Henderson](/wiki/Rickey_Henderson "Rickey Henderson")[Nolan Ryan](/wiki/Nolan_Ryan "Nolan Ryan")[Ryne Sandberg](/wiki/Ryne_Sandberg "Ryne Sandberg") | Themselves | 052–317 | 8F13 | "[Homer at the Bat](/wiki/Homer_at_the_Bat "Homer at the Bat")" |
| **Notes:** These were some of the known baseball stars approached to guest star, only to turn the offer down.[\[340]](#cite_note-340) | | | | | |
| [3](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_3 "The Simpsons season 3") | [Marcia Wallace](/wiki/Marcia_Wallace "Marcia Wallace") | Edna Krabappel | 052–317 | 8F13 | "[Homer at the Bat](/wiki/Homer_at_the_Bat "Homer at the Bat")" |
| **Notes:** Originally guest star [José Canseco](/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Canseco "José Canseco") was slated to wake up in bed with Edna and miss the game in a parody of *[Bull Durham](/wiki/Bull_Durham "Bull Durham")*, but Canseco's then\-wife, Esther Haddad, objected. Edna's part was cut but Wallace had still recorded dialogue and was still credited at the end of the episode.[\[341]](#cite_note-341) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Don Rickles](/wiki/Don_Rickles "Don Rickles") | Himself | 067–408 | 9F06 | "[New Kid on the Block](/wiki/New_Kid_on_the_Block "New Kid on the Block")" |
| **Notes:** The episode's original subplot was intended to include Rickles presenting a comedy show and Homer laughing excessively at his jokes, until Rickles ridicules him. The pair were intended to start fighting and end up having to go to court. Despite production staff being sure that Rickles would appear in the episode, he was reportedly upset by the concept of the storyline, as he did not wish to be portrayed as a "mean guy". When attending a Fox Broadcasting Company publicity event in New York City with [Rupert Murdoch](/wiki/Rupert_Murdoch "Rupert Murdoch"), creator Matt Groening was introduced by Murdoch to Rickles. Rickles began shouting at Groening, accusing him of spying on his Las Vegas act and using material from that for the episode. The cast actually had been receiving recordings from Rickles from the 1950s to use as ideas on how to get the style for his animatic portrayal.[\[342]](#cite_note-newkid-342) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [William Shatner](/wiki/William_Shatner "William Shatner")[George Takei](/wiki/George_Takei "George Takei") | Themselves | 071–412 | 9F10 | "[Marge vs. the Monorail](/wiki/Marge_vs._the_Monorail "Marge vs. the Monorail")" |
| **Notes:** Both *[Star Trek](/wiki/Star_Trek "Star Trek")* actors turned down the role that went to [Leonard Nimoy](/wiki/Leonard_Nimoy "Leonard Nimoy"). Shatner turned down the show for the second time, while Takei only rejected the part because he did not want to make fun of public transportation as he was a member of the board of directors of the [Southern California Rapid Transit District](/wiki/Southern_California_Rapid_Transit_District "Southern California Rapid Transit District") (now the [Los Angeles County Metropolitan Transportation Authority](/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Metropolitan_Transportation_Authority "Los Angeles County Metropolitan Transportation Authority")). Producers had originally skipped asking Nimoy assuming he would turn the part down, only to be pleasantly surprised when he accepted.[\[343]](#cite_note-343) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Tom Cruise](/wiki/Tom_Cruise "Tom Cruise") | Tom | 073–414 | 9F12 | "[Brother from the Same Planet](/wiki/Brother_from_the_Same_Planet "Brother from the Same Planet")" |
| **Notes:** The producers wrote the part of Tom for Cruise having been informed the actor was interested in guest starring, only for him to reject the part when offered. The role was offered to recurring guest [Phil Hartman](/wiki/Phil_Hartman "Phil Hartman") instead.[\[19]](#cite_note-bots-19) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Clint Eastwood](/wiki/Clint_Eastwood "Clint Eastwood")[Anthony Hopkins](/wiki/Anthony_Hopkins "Anthony Hopkins")[Anthony Perkins](/wiki/Anthony_Perkins "Anthony Perkins") | Dr. Wolfe | 076–417 | 9F15 | "[Last Exit to Springfield](/wiki/Last_Exit_to_Springfield "Last Exit to Springfield")" |
| **Notes:** Both Eastwood and Hopkins turned down the role of Dr. Wolfe when offered. Perkins agreed to voice him but died before he could record any lines. The part went to cast member Hank Azaria instead.[\[20]](#cite_note-lets-20) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [O. J. Simpson](/wiki/O._J._Simpson "O. J. Simpson") | Himself | 076–417 | 9F15 | "[Last Exit to Springfield](/wiki/Last_Exit_to_Springfield "Last Exit to Springfield")" |
| **Notes:** Simpson was originally wanted for the guest panelist on Smartline, but he turned down the part that would go to [Dr. Joyce Brothers](/wiki/Dr._Joyce_Brothers "Dr. Joyce Brothers") instead. Producers later stated they were happy it turned out this way given the controversy that would later surround Simpson.[\[20]](#cite_note-lets-20) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Jon Lovitz](/wiki/Jon_Lovitz "Jon Lovitz") | Artie Ziff | 078–419 | 9F16 | "[The Front](/wiki/The_Front_(The_Simpsons) "The Front (The Simpsons)")" |
| **Notes:** Scheduling conflicts prevented Lovitz from being able to reprise his role of Artie Ziff from "[The Way We Was](/wiki/The_Way_We_Was "The Way We Was")" and Dan Castellaneta filled in the role. Lovitz would go on to voice Ziff in the future.[\[344]](#cite_note-thefrontno-344) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Alex Rocco](/wiki/Alex_Rocco "Alex Rocco") | Roger Meyers, Jr. | 078–419 | 9F16 | "[The Front](/wiki/The_Front_(The_Simpsons) "The Front (The Simpsons)")" |
| **Notes:** Scheduling conflicts prevented Rocco from being able to reprise his role of Roger Meyers. Jr. from "[Itchy \& Scratchy \& Marge](/wiki/Itchy_%26_Scratchy_%26_Marge "Itchy & Scratchy & Marge")" and Hank Azaria filled in the role. Azaria would continue to voice the character for the next few seasons, though Rocco did return to the role twice more.[\[344]](#cite_note-thefrontno-344) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Jimmy Carter](/wiki/Jimmy_Carter "Jimmy Carter")[Gerald Ford](/wiki/Gerald_Ford "Gerald Ford")[Wynonna Judd](/wiki/Wynonna_Judd "Wynonna Judd")[Richard Nixon](/wiki/Richard_Nixon "Richard Nixon")[Ronald Reagan](/wiki/Ronald_Reagan "Ronald Reagan")[The Rolling Stones](/wiki/The_Rolling_Stones "The Rolling Stones") | Themselves | 081–422 | 9F19 | "[Krusty Gets Kancelled](/wiki/Krusty_Gets_Kancelled "Krusty Gets Kancelled")" |
| **Notes:** All of these celebrities were approached to guest star as themselves, but all turned down the offer. [Mick Jagger](/wiki/Mick_Jagger "Mick Jagger") and [Keith Richards](/wiki/Keith_Richards "Keith Richards") of The Rolling Stones would later accept guest spots years later in "[How I Spent My Strummer Vacation](/wiki/How_I_Spent_My_Strummer_Vacation "How I Spent My Strummer Vacation")" and their role went to the [Red Hot Chili Peppers](/wiki/Red_Hot_Chili_Peppers "Red Hot Chili Peppers"). Of the then\-living ex\-[President of the United States](/wiki/President_of_the_United_States "President of the United States") offered parts only Reagan actually responded.[\[345]](#cite_note-345) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Prince](/wiki/Prince_(musician) "Prince (musician)") | Leon Kompowsky | ? | ? | Unproduced sequel to "[Stark Raving Dad](/wiki/Stark_Raving_Dad "Stark Raving Dad")" |
| **Notes:** A planned follow\-up to "[Stark Raving Dad](/wiki/Stark_Raving_Dad "Stark Raving Dad")", the plot focused on [Michael Jackson](/wiki/Michael_Jackson "Michael Jackson") impersonator Leon Kompowsky returning to Springfield claiming to be the pop star Prince. The script was written by freelancers and polished by [Conan O'Brien](/wiki/Conan_O%27Brien "Conan O'Brien"). According to executive producer [Mike Reiss](/wiki/Mike_Reiss "Mike Reiss") it saw Kompowsky encourage the Springfield residents to "loosen up, become more flamboyant and become more sexually open". Prince originally agreed to voice Kompowsky, but the writers discovered that Prince was referring to a different script entirely written by a friend of his. Prince disliked their script and demanded the other one be used, but the writers refused and the episode was never produced.[\[346]](#cite_note-346) | | | | | |
| [4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4") | [Frank Zappa](/wiki/Frank_Zappa "Frank Zappa") | Himself | ? | ? | ? |
| **Notes:** Matt Groening and Zappa were friends and mutual fans of each other's work. Zappa had agreed to a guest spot on the show sometime around [season 4](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_4 "The Simpsons season 4"), but became ill and died before he could record a part.[\[347]](#cite_note-347) | | | | | |
| [5](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_5 "The Simpsons season 5") | [Phil Hartman](/wiki/Phil_Hartman "Phil Hartman") | Lionel Hutz | 091–510 | 1F08 | "[$pringfield (Or, How I Learned toStop Worrying and Love Legalized Gambling)](/wiki/$pringfield_(Or,_How_I_Learned_to_Stop_Worrying_and_Love_Legalized_Gambling) "$pringfield (Or, How I Learned to Stop Worrying and Love Legalized Gambling)")" |
| **Notes:** A scene involving Hartman as Lionel Hutz was cut for time. The scene was released as a deleted scene on the DVD set *The Simpsons: The Complete Fifth Season*. | | | | | |
| [5](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_5 "The Simpsons season 5") | [Marvelous Marvin Hagler](/wiki/Marvelous_Marvin_Hagler "Marvelous Marvin Hagler")[Wayne Newton](/wiki/Wayne_Newton "Wayne Newton")[Sylvester Stallone](/wiki/Sylvester_Stallone "Sylvester Stallone")[Arnold Schwarzenegger](/wiki/Arnold_Schwarzenegger "Arnold Schwarzenegger")[Bruce Willis](/wiki/Bruce_Willis "Bruce Willis") | Themselves | 091–510 | 1F08 | "[$pringfield (Or, How I Learned toStop Worrying and Love Legalized Gambling)](/wiki/$pringfield_(Or,_How_I_Learned_to_Stop_Worrying_and_Love_Legalized_Gambling) "$pringfield (Or, How I Learned to Stop Worrying and Love Legalized Gambling)")" |
| **Notes:** Hagler was the original choice for [Gerry Cooney](/wiki/Gerry_Cooney "Gerry Cooney")'s role while Newton was the original pick for [Robert Goulet](/wiki/Robert_Goulet "Robert Goulet")'s, but both rejected the offer. There was a brief period when the episode had a different subplot that revolved around the restaurant chain [Planet Hollywood](/wiki/Planet_Hollywood "Planet Hollywood"). Matt Groening had been told by a spokesperson that if he put Planet Hollywood in *The Simpsons*, Stallone, Schwarzenegger, and Willis, would agree to make guest appearances on the show. The writers of were excited about this so they wrote a new subplot for the episode that featured Planet Hollywood and the three actors. However, for unknown reasons, they were unable to appear in the episode.[\[348]](#cite_note-348) | | | | | |
| [5](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_5 "The Simpsons season 5") | [Michael Caine](/wiki/Michael_Caine "Michael Caine") | Himself | 094–513 | 1F10 | "[Homer and Apu](/wiki/Homer_and_Apu "Homer and Apu")" |
| **Notes:** Caine was the original choice wanted for [James Woods](/wiki/James_Woods "James Woods")' part, but he turned down the offer. It's possible that producers also wanted Caine to voice himself in "[Burns' Heir](/wiki/Burns%27_Heir "Burns' Heir")" that was produced around the same time and only aired five episodes later where Dan Castellaneta did an impression of Caine, but this has not been confirmed. | | | | | |
| [5](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_5 "The Simpsons season 5") | [Richard Simmons](/wiki/Richard_Simmons "Richard Simmons") | Robotic Richard Simmons | 099–518 | 1F16 | "[Burns' Heir](/wiki/Burns%27_Heir "Burns' Heir")" |
| **Notes:** Simmons was interested in a guest spot but rejected the part when he learned he was going to be a robot. The scene was cut from the episode, but a version where the robot was voiced by Dan Castellaneta was shown as a deleted scene in "[The Simpsons 138th Episode Spectacular](/wiki/The_Simpsons_138th_Episode_Spectacular "The Simpsons 138th Episode Spectacular")".[\[349]](#cite_note-349) | | | | | |

| **Notes:** According to [Bill Oakley](/wiki/Bill_Oakley "Bill Oakley"), the then\-Vice President Gore was asked to host the Halloween special in season 6 or 7\.[\[350]](#cite_note-billjoshq&a-350) | | | | | |
| [6](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_6 "The Simpsons season 6") | [Wayne Gretzky](/wiki/Wayne_Gretzky "Wayne Gretzky")[Bobby Orr](/wiki/Bobby_Orr "Bobby Orr") | Themselves | 111–608 | 2F05 | "[Lisa on Ice](/wiki/Lisa_on_Ice "Lisa on Ice")" |

| **Notes:** The producers were unable to get Brinkley to play his part, so he was instead voiced by Harry Shearer.[\[352]](#cite_note-tdtvd-352) | | | | | |

| **Notes:** Hickey was the first choice to voice Chester instead of [Kirk Douglas](/wiki/Kirk_Douglas "Kirk Douglas"), but he rejected the offer.[\[352]](#cite_note-tdtvd-352) | | | | | |
| [7](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_7 "The Simpsons season 7") | [Bob Dylan](/wiki/Bob_Dylan "Bob Dylan")[Bruce Springsteen](/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen "Bruce Springsteen")[Neil Young](/wiki/Neil_Young "Neil Young")[Hole](/wiki/Hole_(band) "Hole (band)")[Pearl Jam](/wiki/Pearl_Jam "Pearl Jam") | Themselves | 152–724 | 3F21 | "[Homerpalooza](/wiki/Homerpalooza "Homerpalooza")" |

| **Notes:** The [Mary Poppins](/wiki/Mary_Poppins_(character) "Mary Poppins (character)") actress was originally touted to play her parody, but Maggie Roswell did such a good job for the temp read that the producers decided to keep it.[\[359]](#cite_note-359) | | | | | |

| **Notes:** Turturro recorded Mr. Mitchell's dialogue, but when he refused to come in again for some re\-takes his audio was cut and Dan Castellaneta re\-voiced the character.[\[360]](#cite_note-360) | | | | | |
| [8](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_8 "The Simpsons season 8") | [Nicolas Cage](/wiki/Nicolas_Cage "Nicolas Cage")[William H. Macy](/wiki/William_H._Macy "William H. Macy")[Steve Martin](/wiki/Steve_Martin "Steve Martin") | Frank Grimes | 176–823 | 4F19 | "[Homer's Enemy](/wiki/Homer%27s_Enemy "Homer's Enemy")" |
| **Notes:** Martin was the original choice for the part of Frank Grimes, but he turned down the offer. He did accept the role of Ray Patterson the very next season in "[Trash of the Titans](/wiki/Trash_of_the_Titans "Trash of the Titans")" however. The role went to Hank Azaria, who based his performance of the character on Macy specifically and thought that Macy should have done it. After his performance at the table reads however the producers decided to keep Azaria as the voice. Macy would eventually guest star in the episode "[Homer's Paternity Coot](/wiki/Homer%27s_Paternity_Coot "Homer's Paternity Coot")".[\[361]](#cite_note-361) Cage was also another consideration until Azara's performance sealed the deal.[\[362]](#cite_note-362) | | | | | |

| **Notes:** Albert was originally going to play [Roy Firestone](/wiki/Roy_Firestone "Roy Firestone")'s part as a sports radio host, but was dropped following [sexual assault charges](/wiki/Marv_Albert#Sexual_assault "Marv Albert") that were made against him around the time the episode was in pre\-production. Albert would later appear in the episode "[The Burns and the Bees](/wiki/The_Burns_and_the_Bees "The Burns and the Bees")".[\[363]](#cite_note-363) | | | | | |

[Archbishop of Canterbury](/wiki/Archbishop_of_Canterbury "Archbishop of Canterbury") Williams was a fan of the show and included in a draft of the script acting as a tour guide and showing some of his relatives around London, but Williams had to reject the part due to other engagements. (song)")" in Korean after the song "[Goldfinger](/wiki/Goldfinger_(Shirley_Bassey_song) "Goldfinger (Shirley Bassey song)")" (that he had originally also learned and recorded in Korean for them) was not allowed to be used on copyright grounds. According to Matt Selman after the trouble they had put him through they felt bad and could not bring themselves to get him back again, so the character of Milo was instead performed by [Maurice LaMarche](/wiki/Maurice_LaMarche "Maurice LaMarche").[\[401]](#cite_note-401) | | | | | |
| [26](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_26 "The Simpsons season 26") | [Joe Mantegna](/wiki/Joe_Mantegna "Joe Mantegna") | Fat Tony | 567–2615 | TABF08 | "[The Princess Guide](/wiki/The_Princess_Guide_(The_Simpsons) "The Princess Guide (The Simpsons)")" |
| **Notes:** Mantegna was touted as reprising his role as Fat Tony in listings for the episode, but did not appear in the final broadcast.[\[402]](#cite_note-402) | | | | | |
| [28](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_28 "The Simpsons season 28") | [Joe Mantegna](/wiki/Joe_Mantegna "Joe Mantegna") | Fat Tony | 602–2806 | VABF22 | "[There Will Be Buds](/wiki/There_Will_Be_Buds "There Will Be Buds")" |
| **Notes:** Mantegna was touted as reprising his role as Fat Tony in listings for the episode, but did not appear in the final broadcast.[\[403]](#cite_note-403) | | | | | |
| [28](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_28 "The Simpsons season 28") | [Drew Barrymore](/wiki/Drew_Barrymore "Drew Barrymore") | Sophie Krustofsky | 606–2810 | WABF02 | "[The Nightmare After Krustmas](/wiki/The_Nightmare_After_Krustmas "The Nightmare After Krustmas")" |
| **Notes:** Barrymore was apparently not interested in returning to the role of Krusty's daughter she originated in "[Insane Clown Poppy](/wiki/Insane_Clown_Poppy "Insane Clown Poppy")" so was replaced by [Natasha Lyonne](/wiki/Natasha_Lyonne "Natasha Lyonne") for this episode and subsequent appearances as well.[\[404]](#cite_note-404) Barrymore would however return to voice herself in the [Season 34](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_34 "The Simpsons season 34") episode "The King of Nice". | | | | | |
| [29](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_29 "The Simpsons season 29") | [George Takei](/wiki/George_Takei "George Takei") | Himself | 628–2910 | XABF03 | "[Haw\-Haw Land](/wiki/Haw-Haw_Land "Haw-Haw Land")" |
| **Notes:** Takei was touted as guest starring in listings for the episode, but did not appear in the final broadcast.[\[405]](#cite_note-405) | | | | | |
| [30](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_30 "The Simpsons season 30") | [Daniel Day Lewis](/wiki/Daniel_Day_Lewis "Daniel Day Lewis") | Himself playing Sideshow Daniel Day Lewis | 657–3018 | YABF10 | "[Bart vs. Itchy \& Scratchy](/wiki/Bart_vs._Itchy_%26_Scratchy "Bart vs. Itchy & Scratchy")" |
| **Notes:** A scene that never made it into the final broadcast would have featured Lewis playing a Sideshow version of himself. He passed and the scene was cut, but there was an animatic of the scene released online by Matt Selman.[\[406]](#cite_note-406) | | | | | |
| [31](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_31 "The Simpsons season 31") | [Werner Herzog](/wiki/Werner_Herzog "Werner Herzog") | Walter Hotenhoffer | 670–3108 | YABF17 | "[Thanksgiving of Horror](/wiki/Thanksgiving_of_Horror "Thanksgiving of Horror")" |
| **Notes:** Herzog was touted as guest starring in what would be his third appearance as Walter Hotenhoffer in listings for the episode, but did not appear in the final broadcast. Herzog would appear later in the season as Dr. Lund in the episode "[Screenless](/wiki/Screenless_(The_Simpsons) "Screenless (The Simpsons)")". | | | | | |
| [32](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_32 "The Simpsons season 32") | [Bill Simmons](/wiki/Bill_Simmons "Bill Simmons") | Himself | 690–3206 | ZABF22 | "[Podcast News](/wiki/Podcast_News "Podcast News")" |
| **Notes:** Simmons was touted as guest starring in listings for the episode, but did not appear in the final broadcast.[\[220]](#cite_note-ZABF22-220) | | | | | |
| [32](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_32 "The Simpsons season 32") | [Larry David](/wiki/Larry_David "Larry David") | Golf\-loving Rabbi | 697–3213 | QABF06 | "[Wad Goals](/wiki/Wad_Goals "Wad Goals")" |
| **Notes:** The producers wanted David for the role but when the [COVID\-19 pandemic](/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic") hit they decided not to bother him.[\[407]](#cite_note-407) | | | | | |
| [33](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_33 "The Simpsons season 33") | [Renee Ridgeley](/wiki/Matt_Selman "Matt Selman") | Dr. Wendy Sage | 724–3318 | UABF11 | "[My Octopus and a Teacher](/wiki/My_Octopus_and_a_Teacher "My Octopus and a Teacher")" |
| **Notes:** Ridgeley was noted as returning to the role of Dr. Sage in this episode but never appeared in the final broadcast.[\[408]](#cite_note-408) | | | | | |
| [34](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_34 "The Simpsons season 34") | [Stephen King](/wiki/Stephen_King "Stephen King") | Magical Gravedigger | 733–3405 | UABF17 | "[Not It](/wiki/Not_It "Not It")" |
| **Notes:** The famous author was asked to make what would have been a second guest stint in this episode parodying of his own novel *[It](/wiki/It_(novel) "It (novel)")* as a magical gravedigger, but he declined.[\[409]](#cite_note-409) | | | | | |
| [35](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_35 "The Simpsons season 35") | [Peter Capaldi](/wiki/Peter_Capaldi "Peter Capaldi") | Unknown Role | 758–3508 | 35ABF02 | "[Ae Bonny Romance](/wiki/Ae_Bonny_Romance "Ae Bonny Romance")" |
| **Notes:** According to writer Michael Price a part was offered to the Scottish actor known for playing the [Twelfth Doctor](/wiki/Twelfth_Doctor "Twelfth Doctor"), but he was unavailable and had to decline.[\[410]](#cite_note-410) | | | | | |
| [36](/wiki/The_Simpsons_season_36 "The Simpsons season 36") | [Barack Obama](/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") | Himself | 769–3601 | 35ABF15 | "[Bart's Birthday](/wiki/Bart%27s_Birthday "Bart's Birthday")" |
| **Notes:** According to co\-showrunner Michael Price a part was written for the former [President of the United States](/wiki/President_of_the_United_States "President of the United States") where different celebrities did tributes to the show and Obama would compare the show to being the president, stating that you begin with eight great years then a lot of bad stuff follows. Obama's representatives passed because he was busy with work related to the [2024 United States presidential election](/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election "2024 United States presidential election").[\[411]](#cite_note-411) | | | | | |
| **Unknown episodes:** | | | | | |
| **Rejected parts:** [Kathy Bates](/wiki/Kathy_Bates "Kathy Bates"),[\[412]](#cite_note-mediareport-412) [George H. W. Bush](/wiki/George_H._W._Bush "George H. W. Bush"), [George W. Bush](/wiki/George_W._Bush "George W. Bush"),[\[413]](#cite_note-413) [Bill Clinton](/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton"),[\[412]](#cite_note-mediareport-412) [Chris Hemsworth](/wiki/Chris_Hemsworth "Chris Hemsworth"), [Liam Hemsworth](/wiki/Liam_Hemsworth "Liam Hemsworth"), [Luke Hemsworth](/wiki/Luke_Hemsworth "Luke Hemsworth"),[\[414]](#cite_note-hemsworths-414) [Barack Obama](/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama"),[\[415]](#cite_note-415) [Gregory Peck](/wiki/Gregory_Peck "Gregory Peck"),[\[342]](#cite_note-newkid-342) [Smokey Robinson](/wiki/Smokey_Robinson "Smokey Robinson"),[\[416]](#cite_note-416) [Bernie Sanders](/wiki/Bernie_Sanders "Bernie Sanders")[\[417]](#cite_note-417) and [Tiger Woods](/wiki/Tiger_Woods "Tiger Woods").[\[418]](#cite_note-418) **Rejected but accepted later:** [Martin Short](/wiki/Martin_Short "Martin Short") rejected a role early in the show's history, but did agree to play Guthrie Frenel later in the episode "[Springfield Splendor](/wiki/Springfield_Splendor "Springfield Splendor")".[\[419]](#cite_note-419)[Hugh Jackman](/wiki/Hugh_Jackman "Hugh Jackman") rejected an unknown role initially, but later agreed to play the Janitor in the episode "[Poorhouse Rock](/wiki/Poorhouse_Rock "Poorhouse Rock")"[\[414]](#cite_note-hemsworths-414) **Rejected by producers:** [Donald Trump](/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump")[\[420]](#cite_note-420) **Other:** * [Robert De Niro](/wiki/Robert_De_Niro "Robert De Niro") was wanted for a part during Bill Oakley and Josh Weinstein's tenure as showrunners (season 7 and 8\) but they never found a role to suit him.[\[350]](#cite_note-billjoshq&a-350) * While discussing successfully getting [Michael Palin](/wiki/Michael_Palin "Michael Palin") for a guest appearance for Season 32 at the Comic\-Con@Home 2020 panel, Al Jean mentioned another member of [Monty Python](/wiki/Monty_Python "Monty Python") had turned them down. While not named it is implied this was likely [John Cleese](/wiki/John_Cleese "John Cleese").[\[421]](#cite_note-421) * In 2021 the staff stated that they had written a part in mind for [Dwayne "The Rock" Johnson](/wiki/Dwayne_%22The_Rock%22_Johnson "Dwayne \"The Rock\" Johnson"), but nothing seemed to have come from it. Yeardley Smith had stated several times that he had been a dream guest star of hers.[\[422]](#cite_note-422) | | | | | |


Notes
-----

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=List_of_The_Simpsons_guest_stars_(seasons_21%E2%80%93present)&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Notes")]

* **[^](#ref_1)** A. This figure counts the members of bands with speaking roles separately. Bands who merely perform a song are counted as one because there is no confirmation of which of the band's members performed on their appearance.
* **[^](#ref_2)** B. Consisting of [Dick Smothers](/wiki/Dick_Smothers "Dick Smothers") and [Tom Smothers](/wiki/Tom_Smothers "Tom Smothers").[\[423]](#cite_note-423)
* **[^](#ref_3)** C. While the entirety of the band [Coldplay](/wiki/Coldplay "Coldplay") were animated playing music in the episode, only Martin had any individual lines and was credited.
* **[^](#ref_4)** D. [Kristen Schaal](/wiki/Kristen_Schaal "Kristen Schaal") was incorrectly credited as "Kristen Schall".[\[424]](#cite_note-424)
* **[^](#ref_5)** E. Kramer had previously guest starred in "[Flaming Moe's](/wiki/Flaming_Moe%27s "Flaming Moe's")" as part of his band [Aerosmith](/wiki/Aerosmith "Aerosmith").
* **[^](#ref_6)** F. While the entirety of the band [The Decemberists](/wiki/The_Decemberists "The Decemberists") were animated playing music in the episode, only Meloy had any individual lines and was credited.
* **[^](#ref_7)** G. Because her sister Janet was suffering from [Parkinson's disease](/wiki/Parkinson%27s_disease "Parkinson's disease") and had trouble delivering some of the longer lines, Maggy substituted for her when needed.
* **[^](#ref_8)** H. Consisting of Aron Michael Manderosian and Robert J. Manderosian.
* **[^](#ref_9)** I. Maines had previously guest starred in "[Papa Don't Leech](/wiki/Papa_Don%27t_Leech "Papa Don't Leech")" as part of her band [The Dixie Chicks](/wiki/The_Dixie_Chicks "The Dixie Chicks").
* **[^](#ref_10)** J. Richardson's last appearance as a guest star before joining the supporting cast the following season.
* **[^](#ref_11)** K. While the entirety of the band [OK Go](/wiki/OK_Go "OK Go") were animated playing music in the episode, only Kulash and Nordwind had any individual lines and were credited.
* **[^](#ref_12)** L. Wallace's appearance consisted of one line of archival audio from the episode "[Bart Gets a "Z"](/wiki/Bart_Gets_a_%22Z%22 "Bart Gets a \"Z\"")", Marcia Wallace having passed away in 2013\.
* **[^](#ref_13)** M. Consisting of Head Ned, Red Ned, Stead Ned, Thread Ned and Bled Ned. While airing in April 2019, the music video used was from 2016 and thus featured former band members rather than the current lineup at the time.
* **[^](#ref_14)** N. Wallace's appearance consisted of archival audio of Edna Krabappel's laugh used in a new scene.
* **[^](#ref_15)** O. Consisting of [Brian Bell](/wiki/Brian_Bell "Brian Bell"), [Rivers Cuomo](/wiki/Rivers_Cuomo "Rivers Cuomo"), [Scott Shriner](/wiki/Scott_Shriner "Scott Shriner") and [Patrick Wilson](/wiki/Patrick_Wilson_(drummer) "Patrick Wilson (drummer)").
* **[^](#ref_16)** P. Wallace's appearance consisted of archival audio of Edna Krabappel used in new scenes.
* **[^](#ref_17)** Q. Anastasio had previously guest starred in "[Weekend at Burnsie's](/wiki/Weekend_at_Burnsie%27s "Weekend at Burnsie's")" as part of his band [Phish](/wiki/Phish "Phish").
* **[^](#ref_18)** R. Mason's appearance consisted of one line of archival audio from the episode "[Clown in the Dumps](/wiki/Clown_in_the_Dumps "Clown in the Dumps")" used in a new scene, Jackie Mason having passed away in 2021\.
